# Known HIDs

Gree's `hid` is a composite firmware identifier. It identifies a firmware target and build, not a unique appliance serial number. The app extracts the numeric firmware code from it when checking for updates ([captured update sequence][update-sequence]). The historical catalog below preserves device reports and separately labelled firmware filenames researched on 2026-10-14. The downloaded-payload inventory adds independently inspected identities, CPU targets, and full SHA-256 hashes; it does not turn an OTA filename into a device observation.

## String segments

For example, `362001066763+U-W06A(P3)V6.04.bin` is a reported Sinclair air-to-water HID associated with MID `9300`. This is identification evidence, not a claim that the current library implements that appliance's full protocol. See [MIDS.md](MIDS.md) for device categories and [OFFSETS.md](../OFFSETS.md) for temperature-decoding evidence.

| Segment | Interpretation | Limits |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `362001066763` | Firmware code, used as `firmwareCode` in Gree's update API. | Treat the complete digit string as an opaque identifier. Its subfields have no verified decoding here, and code lengths vary. |
| `+` | Separator between the numeric code and firmware name. | Not part of the filename prefix. |
| `U-` | Literal prefix within the firmware name. | Its expansion is not verified; some firmware filenames have no such prefix. |
| `W06A` | Module/platform designation. | [INFERENCE] This role follows the naming pattern; individual characters are not decoded. `CS532AE(LT)` is independently documented as a Wi-Fi module name ([Gree certification][module-certification]). |
| `(P3)` | Qualifier attached to the designation. | [INFERENCE] A platform/firmware variant label; no verified expansion of `P3`. It does not establish protocol version 3. Qualifiers such as `LT`, `MTK`, and `MTK2` must not be assumed to have the same meaning. |
| `V6.04` | Firmware version, preserved as the string `6.04`. | Do not convert to a number or compare across firmware families. The communication protocol version is a separate update-API field, `commProtVer`. |
| `.bin` | Binary firmware-image filename extension. | Some download filenames use other naming schemes; not every downloadable image is a reported HID. |

The device's reported `version` extracts the terminal `V<digits/dots>.bin` suffix ([implementation](../greeclimate/device.py)). The [trait matcher](../greeclimate/device_profile.py) parses firmware code, module designation (excluding `U-` and any parenthesized qualifier), and literal version. Every specified profile criterion must match, including MID when present; the first matching profile wins without merging. Current temperature defaults match firmware code only and deliberately extrapolate beyond inspected versions; see [OFFSETS.md](../OFFSETS.md). MID is separate discovery metadata, not an HID segment. A firmware code or CPU target does not prove an appliance model, capabilities, or safe firmware replacement.

## Catalog conventions

- **Reported** means a public device log, status response, or owner's explicit HID report. **Archive** means an identifiable firmware filename associated with a code in the firmware archive. **Gree** means an identifiable filename in the official service's `Content-Disposition` header. **Firmware** means a literal identifier in a published firmware-string dump, not a running-device observation. **Local** in the historical rows means a repository profile at the time of that catalog, not a current support guarantee or an independently verified web report.
- `Unknown` means the checked sources do not establish the value. `None` means that the observed filename has no prefix or parenthesized qualifier, rather than missing evidence.
- Versions are literal HID/identifiable-filename/embedded-identifier suffixes, not arbitrary versions returned by the update API. Server-added numeric filename suffixes are excluded. Generic filenames such as `ota_sdkshell`, bare firmware-code lists, and synthetic test inputs do not establish additional HIDs.
- Multiple values use bullet lists within a cell. These are aggregated observations, **not a Cartesian product**: not every name, module, qualifier, or version combination is a confirmed HID. Follow the row's sources for exact strings and associations.
- Names and MIDs are included only where explicitly associated with the reported HID/code or the local profile. No MID is inferred from an appliance category. This is a sourced snapshot, not an exhaustive registry or a firmware-upgrade compatibility list.

## Evidence notes and exceptions

- Firmware codes are sorted numerically and preserved exactly, without padding. `36300000257` is an 11-digit archive code. Its `CS532RV41` filename is split literally as designation `CS532R` and version `41`; this is **not** a reported HID, and is not normalized to the separately recorded OTA API version `4.1`.
- For `362001065232`, `IC程序` is an extra filename label before `U-`. The table preserves the complete observed filename prefix `IC程序U-`; it does not assert that a device reports that prefix in its HID.
- For `362001065634`, the owner writes the HID as `[362001065634]+U-WB04BRT13V1.44.bin` ([report](https://github.com/home-assistant/core/issues/176486#issuecomment-5217448314)). The table groups the numeric code; the brackets are preserved here as written, and their presence in the device's raw HID is unverified. The report supplies neither an appliance model nor a MID.
- For `362001065279`, the Cooper & Hunter report is associated with received-packet HID `1.05` and MID `10001`, Bora A5 with reported `1.21`, Heiwa Essential ZEN+ with reported `1.23`, and Lomo Nordic with the contributor's archived `1.26` filename. These are not model assignments for every version in the row.
- For `362001068254`, Airy `GWH12AVCXD-K6DNA1A` is reported with `2.09` and MID `10001`; Airy `GWH09AVCXB-K6DNA1B/I` with `2.11`/`2.12`; and Clivia with `2.12`. The latter reports do not supply MIDs. For `362001067541`, the named indoor/outdoor models and code are in a later comment by the same owner who previously supplied the `2.10` HID.
- Issue #176486 also discusses OTA metadata versions `2.01` and `2.09` for `362001067541`, without reporting complete HIDs for those versions ([comment](https://github.com/home-assistant/core/issues/176486#issuecomment-6061164102)). They are not added as observed HID versions.
- For `362001066763`, the same owner reports `6.04` for both Sinclair models and supplies MID `9300` in the linked gree-remote issue. The historical local profile matched that MID and firmware code; it is not present in the current temperature-only profile table. A separately checked official download header names `U-W06A(P3)V6.0-5121008307837131559.bin`; that filename does not replace the reported `6.04`.
- The official service also named `U-BL3332_JDV18-14745487806682515130.bin` for `362001061383` when checked. This supplies the catalog-only `18`, separately from the reported `1`. Links labeled **Gree** point to the metadata endpoint whose image URL supplied the checked filename header; those live responses may change.
- `362001065736` version `3.78` is explicitly **test firmware**. Its embedded identifier proves a firmware artifact exists, not that it was installed, generally released, or is safe to flash.
- Generic-only archive folders such as `36200100812` and `362001063066`, bare known-code lists, and API metadata without an identifiable firmware name were excluded from the historical HID rows: they establish update records, not decodable HIDs. Downloaded artifacts are now accounted for separately below, including opaque packages and non-firmware responses, without inventing reported HIDs.
- Versati reports mentioning MID `9300` and software `V6.04` without a complete HID do not establish the Sinclair firmware-code association; no Versati HID was invented from those fields ([community report][versati-report]).
- For `362001061665`, issue #109230 names `GWH09AGA-K6DNA1A/I` and its linked log reports `2.06`; issue #124007's log supplies `1.21` and `2.01` without a retail model. All three variants have MID `10001` matched to the same device's discovery and status packets. The `362001063794` log supplies MID `10002` but no retail model; nearby U-Crown reports are from other owners and are not assigned to it.
- For `362001065280`, `1.09` is reported by the runtime log in issue #123755, without a same-device MID. Issue #109230 comment 2097199452 explicitly supplies `1.16` and `1.21` with MID `10001`; the row's MID must not be assumed for every version.
- The Home Assistant issue sweep reviewed all 111 `integration: gree` issues and 72 fallback search matches, their 2,840 comments, and all 20 linked `.txt`, `.log`, and `.json` diagnostic attachments found in the labeled issues. Attached logs established identifiers absent from issue text. Screenshots showing only a model or update-UI version were not joined to firmware codes.

## Downloaded-payload inventory

Snapshot: **2026-10-16**, **318 files / 279 distinct SHA-256 payloads**, including 250 payloads beyond the earlier 29-image survey. Every downloaded alias is listed once under its content hash. This inventory includes executable firmware, multi-image/application packages, opaque updates, and non-firmware responses; a `.bin` extension does not prove executable content.

**SHA-256 hashes the complete downloaded bytes**, including any archive/container wrapper, not a normalized filename or just the extracted application. Different hashes remain separate even when their generated HID is identical. Where extraction reveals a different member identity, the row retains the outer hash and explains the member. Source URLs and API responses can change; the hash identifies the inspected snapshot, not an update recommendation.

The historical reports above and these downloaded images are separate evidence sets. A reported HID without its matching binary has no established SHA. Download names, API versions, and test-server labels are not reported HIDs. The **recovered identity** column distinguishes active construction, compiled fallback/default, manifest-only identity, and runtime formats in its expandable evidence. None is an observation of an installed appliance. Unknown identities remain unknown; no retail model is inferred.

Boot/debug labels and companion identifiers such as `InHid` or `BtHid` are not substitutes for the primary `hid` response. For example, the 066542 gateway's companion builder names 066543, while its actual primary HID callback reports `362001066542+U-W05G(RELT)V1.3.bin`. Identity evidence must follow the selected property handler, not merely find a plausible firmware string.

### CPU evidence

CPU entries distinguish decoded instruction architecture, compiler/SDK/board target, and exact fitted silicon. Shared driver paths, module names, and web-interface assets alone do not identify a physical chip. In particular, an `(MTK)` fallback HID occurs in Xtensa firmware; shared QCA4010 web assets do not prove a QCA4010 chip; and RTL8710C, RTL8710B, and RTL8721D SDK families must not be conflated.

Family references: [Realtek RTL8710BN / Cortex-M4](https://www.realtek.com/Product/Index?cate_id=194&id=625), [AmebaZ2 RTL8710C-family / Cortex-M33](https://docs.libretiny.eu/docs/platform/realtek-ambz2/), [AmebaD KM4/KM0](https://www.amebaiot.com/en/amebad/), and [MediaTek MT7687F / Cortex-M4](https://www.mediatek.com/products/home-networking/mt7687f). These explain family terminology; per-row binary evidence establishes the inspected target. Exact package/model stays unknown where not established.

### Payloads

Rows are sorted by recovered numeric firmware code where available, otherwise the download code, then identity and hash. Addresses in evidence are mapped virtual addresses unless marked as file offsets. Temperature conclusions and blockers are linked to [OFFSETS.md](../OFFSETS.md); positive MID routing/default/emission evidence is in [MIDS.md](MIDS.md#firmware-mid-evidence).

The firmware MID column lists recovered default, emitted, or routed identifiers; it does not assign every listed ID to the host device. Follow **Roles / conditions** for the exact per-payload evidence in MIDS.md. `Unknown` means no fixed MID was recovered, not that the device has no MID. Historical reported MIDs above are not copied onto unverified downloaded builds.
